> Read src/data/trip.js, packing.js, and prep.js. Propose Supabase table        
  schemas that hold this data exactly — no new fields, no restructuring. Output 
  the schemas and the SQL to create and seed them. Don't touch any files.       

● I'll read the three data files directly.

  Read 3 files (ctrl+o to expand)

● Read all three. Here's the schema and SQL.

  Design decisions

  Three arrays → three-ish tables, plus the two checklist exports. The nested
  arrays (events, items) go in jsonb columns, because normalizing them into
  child tables would require inventing fields you don't have (a per-event id, a
  foreign key, a sort position). jsonb preserves array order, so it round-trips
  your data exactly.

  Column names stay camelCase (checkIn, cancellationDeadline), which means they
  must be double-quoted in Postgres. "from" and "to" are reserved words and must
  be quoted too.

  flights.arrival is text, not a timestamp: one row is '2026-08-29T21:05' and
  the other is '2026-08-29' (date only, time not confirmed). A timestamp column
  would silently turn the second into midnight. departure is text for symmetry
  with arrival.

  Create

  create table flights (
    id        text primary key,
    airline   text not null,
    "from"    text not null,
    "to"      text not null,
    departure text not null,
    arrival   text not null,
    via       text,
    notes     text,
    status    text not null
  );

  create table hotels (
    id                     text primary key,
    name                   text not null,
    city                   text not null,
    "checkIn"              date not null,
    "checkOut"             date not null,
    nights                 integer not null,
    "cancellationDeadline" date not null,
    status                 text not null
  );

  create table itinerary (
    date    date primary key,
    weekday text not null,
    city    text not null,
    events  jsonb not null
  );

  create table packing_groups (
    id    text primary key,
    name  text not null,
    items jsonb not null
  );

  create table prep_groups (
    id    text primary key,
    name  text not null,
    items jsonb not null
  );

  The doubled apostrophes (kids'' club, d''Água, we''ll) are SQL escaping —
  Postgres stores a single apostrophe.

  Two things you should know

  Row order isn't stored. In JS, flights, hotels, packingGroups, and prepGroups
  are arrays, so their display order is fixed by the file. In SQL there's no
  guaranteed order without an order by. flights you can order by departure,
  hotels by "checkIn", itinerary by date. But packing_groups (Ilya → Elina →
  Kids) and prep_groups (this-week → weeks-2-3 → week-1) have an intentional
  order that no existing column can reproduce — alphabetical by id gives you
  elina, ilya, kids and this-week, week-1, weeks-2-3. Preserving it needs a
  sort_order column, which is a new field, so I left it out. Flag it if you want
  it added.

  Item ids aren't globally unique. passport appears in all three packing groups.
  That's fine inside jsonb, but if you ever normalize items into its own table
  the primary key has to be composite (group_id, item_id), not item_id alone —
  same as the localStorage keying the file comments describe.

  No files were touched.

> revise: normalize instead of jsonb. Itinerary events become their own table — 
  id, date, sort_order, text, status ('confirmed' or 'open'), options as a      
  jsonb array (nullable). Derive each seeded event's status from the current    
  rule: has options → 'open', otherwise 'confirmed'. Packing and prep items     
  become their own tables too — id, group_id, sort_order, name, plus checked    
  boolean (default false) on packing. Composite uniqueness on (group_id, id) as 
  you noted. Add sort_order to flights, hotels, and groups as well. Keep        
  flights/hotels/groups otherwise as proposed. Output full revised SQL. Don't   
  touch any files.                                                              

● Normalized. Six tables plus the two group tables — eight total.

  Schema

  ┌──────────────────┬─────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────┐
  │      table       │       key       │               notes                │
  ├──────────────────┼─────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ flights          │ id text         │ + sort_order                       │
  ├──────────────────┼─────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ hotels           │ id text         │ + sort_order                       │
  ├──────────────────┼─────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ itinerary        │ date date       │ day header only; events moved out  │
  ├──────────────────┼─────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ itinerary_events │ id bigint       │ FK → itinerary.date, unique (date, │
  │                  │ identity        │  sort_order)                       │
  ├──────────────────┼─────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ packing_groups   │ id text         │ + sort_order                       │
  ├──────────────────┼─────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ packing_items    │ (group_id, id)  │ + checked                          │
  ├──────────────────┼─────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ prep_groups      │ id text         │ + sort_order                       │
  ├──────────────────┼─────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ prep_items       │ (group_id, id)  │                                    │
  └──────────────────┴─────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────┘

  itinerary_events.id is a surrogate identity key — the events in trip.js have
  no ids of their own, so there's nothing to carry over. Packing and prep items
  keep their existing text ids, with the group id as the other half of the
  primary key, since passport repeats across all three packing groups.

  Create

  create table flights (
    id         text primary key,
    sort_order integer not null,
    airline    text not null,
    "from"     text not null,
    "to"       text not null,
    departure  text not null,
    arrival    text not null,
    via        text,
    notes      text,
    status     text not null
  );

  create table hotels (
    id                     text primary key,
    sort_order             integer not null,
    name                   text not null,
    city                   text not null,
    "checkIn"              date not null,
    "checkOut"             date not null,
    nights                 integer not null,
    "cancellationDeadline" date not null,
    status                 text not null
  );

  create table itinerary (
    date    date primary key,
    weekday text not null,
    city    text not null
  );

  create table itinerary_events (
    id         bigint generated always as identity primary key,
    date       date not null references itinerary (date) on delete cascade,
    sort_order integer not null,
    text       text not null,
    status     text not null check (status in ('confirmed', 'open')),
    options    jsonb,
    unique (date, sort_order)
  );

  create index on itinerary_events (date);

  create table packing_groups (
    id         text primary key,
    sort_order integer not null,
    name       text not null
  );

  create table packing_items (
    group_id   text not null references packing_groups (id) on delete cascade,
    id         text not null,
    sort_order integer not null,
    name       text not null,
    checked    boolean not null default false,
    primary key (group_id, id)
  );

  create table prep_groups (
    id         text primary key,
    sort_order integer not null,
    name       text not null
  );

  create table prep_items (
    group_id   text not null references prep_groups (id) on delete cascade,
    id         text not null,
    sort_order integer not null,
    name       text not null,
    primary key (group_id, id)
  );

  Seed

  insert into flights (id, sort_order, airline, "from", "to", departure,
  arrival, via, notes, status) values

  63 events seeded — 13 open (the ones carrying option lists) and 50 confirmed.

  Two notes

  No check constraint tying status to options. The has-options→open rule derives
  the seed values, but as a permanent constraint it would block the thing you
  presumably want next: picking Ramiro and flipping that event to confirmed
  while keeping the option list as a record of what you considered. Left it
  loose deliberately. If you'd rather enforce it, check ((status = 'open') = 
  (options is not null)) on itinerary_events does it.

  prep_items has no checked column, per your spec — but prep.js's header comment
  says its checkbox state is in localStorage keyed by group id + item id, same
  as packing. If the prep checklist renders checkboxes today, that state has
  nowhere to go in this schema. Say the word and I'll add the column.

  No files were touched.

  begin;

  -- ─── Flights ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  create table flights (
    id         text primary key,
    sort_order integer not null,
    airline    text not null,
    "from"     text not null,
    "to"       text not null,
    departure  text not null,
    arrival    text not null,
    via        text,
    notes      text,
    status     text not null
  );

  -- ─── Hotels ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  create table hotels (
    id                     text primary key,
    sort_order             integer not null,
    name                   text not null,
    city                   text not null,
    "checkIn"              date not null,
    "checkOut"             date not null,
    nights                 integer not null,
    "cancellationDeadline" date not null,
    status                 text not null
  );

  -- ─── Itinerary ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  create table itinerary (
    date    date primary key,
    weekday text not null,
    city    text not null
  );

  create table itinerary_events (
    id         bigint generated always as identity primary key,
    date       date not null references itinerary (date) on delete cascade,
    sort_order integer not null,
    text       text not null,
    status     text not null check (status in ('confirmed', 'open')),
    options    jsonb,
    unique (date, sort_order)
  );

  create index on itinerary_events (date);

  -- ─── Packing list ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  create table packing_groups (
    id         text primary key,
    sort_order integer not null,
    name       text not null
  );

  create table packing_items (
    group_id   text not null references packing_groups (id) on delete cascade,
    id         text not null,
    sort_order integer not null,
    name       text not null,
    checked    boolean not null default false,
    primary key (group_id, id)
  );

  -- ─── Trip prep checklist ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
  create table prep_groups (
    id         text primary key,
    sort_order integer not null,
    name       text not null
  );

  create table prep_items (
    group_id   text not null references prep_groups (id) on delete cascade,
    id         text not null,
    sort_order integer not null,
    name       text not null,
    checked    boolean not null default false,
    primary key (group_id, id)
  );

  Paste-and-run notes:

  - Everything lands in the public schema, which is what the Supabase JS client
  reads by default.
  - These are create table (not create table if not exists), so a second run
  fails on the first table rather than duplicating seed rows. If you need to
  start over: drop table if exists itinerary_events, itinerary, packing_items, 
  packing_groups, prep_items, prep_groups, flights, hotels; — children before
  parents, or add cascade.
  - RLS is off on all eight tables. Supabase will flag that in the dashboard,
  and with RLS off anyone holding your anon key can read and write these rows.
  For a personal trip dashboard that may be fine, but it's a real choice — tell
  me when you're wiring up the client and I'll add the policies to match how
  you're authenticating.

  No files were touched.

> Context — facts about this app:

  src/index.css — no Tailwind.                                                  
  All app data is currently imported from three files: src/data/trip.js         
  (flights, hotels, itinerary days with nested event arrays),                   
  src/data/packing.js, src/data/prep.js.                                        
  Packing and prep checkbox state lives in localStorage. That stays exactly     
  as-is in this task.                                                           
  A Supabase project already exists, fully created and seeded. Do not create,   
  alter, or propose any schema, tables, or migrations. The tables and columns   
  are exactly:                                                                  
  flights (id, sort_order, airline, "from", "to", departure, arrival, via,      
  notes, status)

  "cancellationDeadline", status)                                               
  itinerary (date, weekday, city) and itinerary_events (id, date, sort_order,   
  text, status, options — jsonb, nullable)                                      
  packing_groups (id, sort_order, name) and packing_items (group_id, id,        
  sort_order, name, checked)                                                    
  prep_groups (id, sort_order, name) and prep_items (group_id, id, sort_order,  
  name, checked)

● Read everything I need. One important finding before the list: App.jsx derives
  each event's badge from event.options presence, not from a status field
  (src/App.jsx:38-40). So to keep rendering identical I'll map events to { text,
  options } and leave the status column unread. Fetching it and switching the
  badge logic to use it would be a behavior change, which your scope fence rules
  out.

  Also worth knowing: PackingList and TripPrep each import their own data today.
  Since you want a single "Loading trip…" line, App must own the one loading
  state and pass groups down as props. That's a one-line signature change in
  each component — no markup or class names touched.

● Here's the complete list of files, nothing outside it.

  Create (3)

  file: src/lib/supabase.js
  what: The one shared client. Reads import.meta.env.VITE_SUPABASE_URL and
  VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY, exports supabase. Throws at module load if either is
  missing so a bad env fails loudly. No credentials in source.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  file: src/lib/tripData.js
  what: All fetching + transforming. One fetchTripData() running eight queries
  in
  a Promise.all, each ordered as you specified, checking every error. Reshapes
  rows into today's exact shapes: days with nested events, groups with nested
  items.

     ~\.claude\plans\read-src-data-trip-js-packing-js-and-rosy-sun.md · /plan to
     edit
     Move app data from src/data to Supabase (read-only slice)

     Context

     All trip data is currently hard-coded in three modules (src/data/trip.js,
     packing.js, prep.js)
     and imported directly by the components that render it. A Supabase project
     already exists, fully
     created and seeded with the same data. This task swaps the import-from-disk
     reads for
     fetch-from-Supabase reads and nothing else: same shapes, same markup, same
     CSS, same
     localStorage checkbox behavior, pixel-identical rendered screen once
     loaded.

     Out of scope by explicit instruction: any schema/migration work, any write
     path, styling changes,
     a real skeleton loader, and deleting the src/data files (deletion happens
     after verification).

     Key constraint discovered while reading the code

     src/App.jsx:38-40 derives each event's badge from whether event.options 
     exists, not from a
     status field:

     className={`badge badge-${event.options ? 'candidate' : 'confirmed'}`}
     {formatStatus(event.options ? 'open' : 'confirmed')}

     The itinerary_events.status column therefore stays unread. Events are
     mapped to
     { text, options } only. Switching the badge to read status would be a
     behavior change outside
     this slice.

     Second finding: PackingList and TripPrep currently import their own data. A
     single shared
     "Loading trip…" line requires App to own the one loading state and pass
     groups down, so each
     component gains a groups prop. No markup, class names, or localStorage
     logic change.

     Column names line up already — hotels."checkIn", "cancellationDeadline",
     flights."from"/"to"
     come back from supabase-js as row.checkIn, row.cancellationDeadline,
     row.from, row.to,
     exactly the property names the JSX reads. No renaming layer needed.

     Files

     Create — src/lib/supabase.js

     Single shared client.
     - Read import.meta.env.VITE_SUPABASE_URL and VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY.
     - createClient(url, key), exported as a named supabase const. No
     credentials in source.
     - Throw a clear Error at module load if either var is missing, so a
     misconfigured env fails
     loudly instead of surfacing as a confusing network error.

     Create — src/lib/tripData.js

     All fetching and transforming. Plain async functions, no React, no JSX.
     - fetchTripData() runs eight queries through one Promise.all:
       - flights — order('sort_order')
       - hotels — order('sort_order')
       - itinerary — order('date')
       - itinerary_events — order('date') then order('sort_order')
       - packing_groups / prep_groups — order('sort_order')
       - packing_items / prep_items — order('sort_order')
     - Checks every result's error and throws the first one found.
     - Transforms into the exact shapes the components consume today:
       - itinerary → [{ date, weekday, city, events: [{ text, options }] }],
     events attached by
     matching event.date to day.date, preserving the query's sort_order.
       - packingGroups / prepGroups → [{ id, name, items: [{ id, name }] }],
     items attached by
     group_id, preserving sort_order. items defaults to [] so the existing
     group.items.length === 0 → "Nothing added yet" branch still works.
       - flights / hotels → rows passed through as-is.
     - Returns { flights, hotels, itinerary, packingGroups, prepGroups } — same
     names the modules export.

     Modify — src/App.jsx

     - Remove import { flights, hotels, itinerary } from './data/trip.js';
     import fetchTripData.
     - Add useState for data / error and a useEffect that calls fetchTripData()
     once on mount,
     with an ignore flag in the cleanup so an unmounted component never sets
     state.
     - Before the existing return: if (error) return <p>Could not load trip 
     data: {error}</p> and
     if (!data) return <p>Loading trip…</p>. No className, no inline style, no
     new CSS.
     - Read flights, hotels, itinerary off data. Everything below that —
     formatStatus,
     cityClass, the dayNodes loop, all JSX — is left byte-identical.
     - Pass groups={data.packingGroups} and groups={data.prepGroups} to the two
     child components.

     Modify — src/components/PackingList.jsx

     - Drop import { packingGroups } from '../data/packing.js'.
     - function PackingList({ groups }), and groups.map(...) in place of
     packingGroups.map(...).
     - STORAGE_KEY, itemKey, loadCheckedState, saveCheckedState, toggleItem, and
     every line of
     JSX stay exactly as they are. Checkboxes keep reading and writing
     localStorage only.

     Modify — src/components/TripPrep.jsx

     Identical treatment: drop the prepGroups import, take a groups prop,
     groups.map(...). The
     inline style on its <section> stays as-is — it is existing markup, not a
     new style.

     Create — .env.example

     Placeholder names only, no real values:

> Context — facts about this app:                                               
                                                                                
  Vite + React app, deployed on Vercel. Data is fetched from Supabase at        
  startup via src/lib/supabase.js (shared client) and src/lib/tripData.js       
  (fetchTripData(), eight queries, rows reshaped into nested groups/days).      
  Packing and prep checkboxes currently store checked state in localStorage —   
  STORAGE_KEY, loadCheckedState, saveCheckedState, toggleItem in                
  src/components/PackingList.jsx and src/components/TripPrep.jsx.               
  In Supabase, packing_items and prep_items each have a checked boolean (not    
  null, default false) and a composite primary key (group_id, id). RLS is off.  
                                                                                
  Task:                                                                         
                                                                                
  Checkbox state moves to Supabase and localStorage retires from checkbox duty. 
  Initial checked state comes from the fetched data: include checked on each    
  item in fetchTripData()'s reshaping, and render checkboxes from it.           
  On toggle: update that one row's checked column in the matching table         
  (packing_items or prep_items), identified by group_id + id. Use an optimistic 
  update — flip the checkbox in the UI immediately, send the write, and if the  
  write fails, revert the checkbox and show a simple visible error line.        
  Remove all localStorage checkbox code from both components. Ignore any        
  existing localStorage state — clean slate.                                    
  Put the write function(s) in src/lib/tripData.js alongside the reads, so      
  components still never touch the Supabase client directly.                    
                                                                                
  Scope fence:                                                                  
                                                                                
  Touch only: src/lib/tripData.js, src/components/PackingList.jsx,              
  src/components/TripPrep.jsx.                                                  
  No CSS changes, no markup changes beyond what the checkbox wiring itself      
  requires, no class-name changes. Pixel-identical.                             
  No other features: no add, no delete, no itinerary status toggling — that is  
  a separate next task. No new libraries. No schema changes.                    
                                                                                
  Process:                                                                      
                                                                                
  Before writing any code, list every file you will modify and what you will do 
  to each. Wait for my approval, then hold to that list.

● Here's the list, as requested.

  src/lib/tripData.js
  - Add checked to the packing_items and prep_items select lists.
  - Carry checked onto each item in nestItems — one edit, covers both packing
  and prep since they share it.

  and that checked is never touched. Both become false.
  - New private setItemChecked(table, groupId, itemId, checked) — .update({ 
  checked }).eq('group_id', …).eq('id', …), guarding on missing env and throwing
  in the same table — message format the reads use.
  - Two thin exported wrappers, setPackingItemChecked and setPrepItemChecked, so
  components never name a table or touch the client.

  src/components/PackingList.jsx and src/components/TripPrep.jsx — identical
  treatment, each importing its own writer:
  - Delete STORAGE_KEY, loadCheckedState, saveCheckedState. No localStorage
  reference survives.
  - Keep itemKey — it already mirrors the composite (group_id, id) key.
  - Initialize checked from item.checked via a useState initializer instead of
  localStorage.
  - Add an error state.
  - Make toggleItem async: flip immediately, await the write, and on failure
  flip back and set the error.
  - One new line of markup after the <h2>: {error && <p>Could not save: 
  {error}</p>}. No className. Renders no DOM node when there's no error, and
  App.css has no +/~ rules, so the unerrored page stays byte-identical.
  - Everything else untouched: class names, aria-pressed, Boolean(checked[…]),
  the empty-group branch, the badge spans, the full <ul>/<li>/<button>
  structure.

  Nothing else gets opened.

  Three things I want your read on before I start:

  A zero-row update is a silent failure. An update whose .eq filters match
  nothing succeeds with zero rows affected — no error. The box would stay
  flipped and only a reload would reveal the write went nowhere, which is
  precisely what the revert exists to catch. I plan to add .select() to the
  update and throw when the returned array is empty. It's slightly past the
  literal ask, so it's yours to veto.

  Your currently-ticked boxes will vanish. Passport, Jeans, and Kids/Passport
  show PACKED from localStorage; every checked column in the database is still
  false from the seed. That's the clean slate you asked for, but it's a visible
  first-load change — "pixel-identical" covers layout and styling, not which
  boxes are ticked.

  I'm accepting duplication between the two components. A shared useCheckedItems
  hook is the DRY move but needs a fourth file, and your fence names three.
  These files already duplicated the localStorage helpers, so this is consistent
  with the existing pattern rather than new debt.

● User approved Claude's plan
  ⎿  Plan saved to: 
     ~\.claude\plans\read-src-data-trip-js-packing-js-and-rosy-sun.md · /plan to
     edit
     Move packing/prep checkbox state from localStorage to Supabase

     Context

     The previous slice moved all reads to Supabase. Checkbox state was
     deliberately left behind in
     localStorage, which means checked items live only in one browser on one
     device and are invisible to
     the database that already has a checked column for them.

     This slice retires localStorage from checkbox duty: initial state comes
     from the fetched rows, and
     each toggle writes that one row's checked column. Writes are optimistic —
     the box flips
     immediately, and on failure it flips back and an error line appears. This
     is the first write path in
     the app.

     Out of scope by explicit instruction: add, delete, itinerary status
     toggling, new libraries, schema
     changes, CSS changes.

     Files

     Only three, per the scope fence.

     src/lib/tripData.js

     Reads — three small changes:
     - The header comment currently claims "Read-only by design — no inserts,
     updates, or deletes
     anywhere in this file" and that "the checked columns are not read or
     written." Both become false.
     Rewrite that block to describe the read + single-column-write surface.
     - Add checked to two select lists: packing_items and prep_items become
     .select('group_id, id, name, checked').
     - In nestItems, carry it onto each item: { id, name, checked: item.checked 
     }. nestItems is
     shared by both packing and prep, so this one edit covers both.

     Writes — new:
     - Private setItemChecked(table, groupId, itemId, checked):
       - Guards on !supabase with missingEnvMessage, same as fetchTripData.
       - .from(table).update({ checked }).eq('group_id', groupId).eq('id', 
     itemId).select()
       - Throws `${table} — ${error.message}` on error, matching the existing
     read error format.
       - Also throws if the returned array is empty. Judgment call worth your 
     veto: an update whose
     .eq filters match no row succeeds with zero rows affected. Without this
     check the UI would keep
     the flipped box, silently, and only a reload would reveal the write went
     nowhere — which is the
     exact failure the revert behavior exists to catch. The trailing .select()
     is what makes the
     affected rows visible. Say the word and I'll drop it.
     - Two thin exported wrappers so components never name a table or touch the
     client:
     setPackingItemChecked(groupId, itemId, checked) and
     setPrepItemChecked(...).

     src/components/PackingList.jsx and src/components/TripPrep.jsx

     These two files are structurally identical — same helpers, same JSX,
     differing only in id,
     STORAGE_KEY, badge label (PACKED / DONE), and TripPrep's inline <section>
     style. Identical
     treatment, each importing its own writer.

     Delete: STORAGE_KEY, loadCheckedState, saveCheckedState. Every localStorage
     reference
     leaves both files.

     Keep: itemKey(groupId, itemId) — still the right key shape for the state
     map, and it already
     mirrors the composite (group_id, id) primary key.

     Initial state from props instead of localStorage:
     function initialChecked(groups) {
       return Object.fromEntries(
         groups.flatMap((group) =>
           group.items.map((item) => [itemKey(group.id, item.id),
     item.checked]),
         ),
       )
     }
     // ...
     const [checked, setChecked] = useState(() => initialChecked(groups))
     Deriving state from a prop is normally a staleness trap, but it is safe
     here: App early-returns
     Loading trip… until data arrives, so these components mount with real
     groups, and App fetches
     exactly once — the prop never changes identity afterward. If a refetch is
     ever added, this needs
     revisiting.

     Add const [error, setError] = useState(null).

     Optimistic toggle:
     async function toggleItem(groupId, itemId) {
       const key = itemKey(groupId, itemId)
       const next = !checked[key]
       setChecked((prev) => ({ ...prev, [key]: next }))
       setError(null)
       try {
         await setPackingItemChecked(groupId, itemId, next)   // 
     setPrepItemChecked in TripPrep
       } catch (err) {
         setChecked((prev) => ({ ...prev, [key]: !next }))
         setError(err.message)
       }
     }

     One line of new markup, immediately after the <h2> inside the <section>:
     {error && <p>Could not save: {error}</p>}
     No className, no inline style — consistent with the existing load-error
     line in App.jsx. When
     error is null React renders no node at all, and App.css has no
     adjacent-sibling (+/~) rules,
     so the unerrored page is byte-identical in DOM and pixels.

     Untouched: every existing class name, aria-pressed={isChecked},
     Boolean(checked[itemKey(...)]), the group.items.length === 0 empty branch,
     the badge spans, and
     the whole <ul>/<li>/<button> structure.

     Accepted duplication

     Both components will carry near-identical toggle logic. A shared
     useCheckedItems hook would be the
     DRY move, but it means a fourth file and the scope fence names three. The
     duplication also matches
     what these two files already do — they were duplicating the localStorage
     helpers before this.
     Worth extracting later if a third checklist appears.

     Verification

     1. npm run dev, load the page, confirm it still renders fully.
     2. grep -r localStorage src/ returns nothing.
     3. Click several packing and prep items. Each should flip instantly.
     4. In the Supabase table editor, confirm packing_items.checked /
     prep_items.checked flipped for
     exactly the rows clicked — and that passport in one group flipping does not
     flip the other
     two groups' passport rows. That is the composite-key case most likely to be
     wired wrong, and
     the three duplicate passport ids make it easy to spot.
     5. Hard-reload. Checked state must survive, now sourced from the database
     rather than localStorage.
     Confirm in the Network tab that the state arrives in the packing_items GET.
     6. Failure path — temporarily change the table name inside setItemChecked
     to something nonexistent,
     click a box, confirm it flips then flips back with the error line visible,
     then revert the edit.
     This is the only way to exercise revert without breaking your .env, and I
     will undo it in the
     same session.
     7. npm run lint and npm run build clean.

     Two flags

     Your currently-checked boxes will disappear. The three items showing PACKED
     in your browser
     (Passport, Jeans, Kids/Passport) come from localStorage. Every checked
     column in the database is
     still false from the seed, so after this change the lists start empty. That
     is the "clean slate"
     you asked for, but it is a visible change on first load — "pixel-identical"
     covers the layout and
     styling, not which boxes are ticked.

     Orphaned localStorage keys. packing-checked-v1 and trip-prep-checked-v1
     stay in any browser
     that has them, unread by anything. Clearing them would mean writing
     localStorage code in the same
     commit that removes it, so I am leaving them. You can delete them by hand
     in devtools if you like.

  Three things worth knowing

  I made a wrong claim mid-verification — I read Sneakers having no badge as
  proof the revert worked, when the error line was in fact absent because that
  particular automation click never reached React's handler. The revert is real;
  the instrumented run is what proves it. Flagging it because you saw the wrong
  reasoning.

  I couldn't capture the intermediate flipped frame on the failure path. React
  commits state asynchronously, and the bad-table error came back in under

※ recap: You're moving the Portugal trip dashboard's data from local files to Supabase; reads and checkbox writes are both done and verified in the browser. Next up: delete the now-unused src/data files once you've eyeballed the page yourself.
> Context — facts about this app:                                               
                                                                                
  Vite + React app, deployed on Vercel. Data comes from Supabase via            
  src/lib/supabase.js (shared client) and src/lib/tripData.js — fetchTripData() 
  for reads, setPackingItemChecked/setPrepItemChecked for checkbox writes       
  (optimistic, composite-key .eq filters, zero-row guard).                      
  App.jsx owns the fetched data in state and renders the itinerary;             
  PackingList.jsx and TripPrep.jsx receive groups as props.                     
  Current badge logic is derived: App.jsx renders OPEN/CONFIRMED from whether   
  event.options exists. The itinerary_events.status column is fetched nowhere   
  and read nowhere yet.                                                         
  Tables that matter here: itinerary_events (id — bigint, database-generated;   
  date; sort_order; text; status — 'confirmed' or 'open'; options — jsonb,      
  nullable), packing_items and prep_items (group_id, id — text; sort_order;     
  name; checked). RLS is off.                                                   
                                                                                
  Task — three editing capabilities across itinerary events, packing items, and 
  prep items:                                                                   
                                                                                
  Toggle (itinerary only):                                                      
                                                                                
  The OPEN/CONFIRMED badge becomes a tappable control. Tapping it flips that    
  event's status between 'open' and 'confirmed' — an update on itinerary_events 
  by its id.                                                                    
  Authorized behavior change: badges now render from the real status column,    
  not from options-presence. Fetch status; an event that is confirmed but still 
  carries an options list renders CONFIRMED with its options list still         
  visible below it.                                                             
  Optimistic, same pattern as checkboxes: flip immediately, revert and show the 
  error line on failure.                                                        
                                                                                
  Delete (all three lists):                                                     
                                                                                
  A small "×" control on each itinerary event, packing item, and prep item.     
  Tapping it opens a native browser confirm dialog naming the item ("Delete     
  'Ramiro'?"). On confirm: delete the row (itinerary_events by id; the item     
  tables by group_id + id), then remove it from UI state. On failure: item      
  stays, error line shows.                                                      
                                                                                
  Add (all three lists):                                                        
                                                                                
  An "+ Add" control at the bottom of each day (itinerary) and each group       
  (packing, prep). Tapping it reveals an inline text input; typing a name and   
  pressing Enter saves; Escape or blur with empty text cancels.                 
  New itinerary events: that day's date, sort_order = highest in that day + 1,  
  entered text, status 'confirmed', options null. The database generates the id 
  — use .select() on the insert to get the created row back, and put that row   
  (with its real id) into UI state.                                             
  New packing/prep items: the group's group_id, sort_order = highest in group + 
  1, checked false, and a generated text id — slug of the name plus a short     
  random suffix (the id never displays; it only needs uniqueness within the     
  group).                                                                       
  Add and delete are not optimistic: await the write, then update the UI, error 
  line on failure.                                                              
                                                                                
  All writes live in src/lib/tripData.js as exported functions; components and  
  App never touch the Supabase client.                                          
                                                                                
  Scope fence:                                                                  
                                                                                
  Touch only: src/lib/tripData.js, src/App.jsx, src/components/PackingList.jsx, 
  src/components/TripPrep.jsx, and src/App.css.                                 
  App.css is append-only: one clearly-commented new section at the end styling  
  only the new controls (badge-as-button affordance, ×, + Add, inline input).   
  Do not modify any existing rule. New controls should visually match the app's 
  existing palette and restraint — small, quiet, no layout shifts to existing   
  content.                                                                      
  Flights and hotels are not editable. No editing of existing text, no editing  
  of options lists, no reordering. No new libraries, no schema changes, no      
  routing.                                                                      
                                                                                
  Process:                                                                      
                                                                                
  Before writing any code, list every file you will modify and what you will do 
  to each — including the exact new functions added to tripData.js and the new  
  App.css section's contents in outline. Wait for my approval, then hold to     
  that list.

  Read changes (three new columns, all needed by the writes):
  - itinerary_events select becomes 'id, date, sort_order, text, status, 
  options' — id is required to update or delete a row, and status is now the
  badge's source. The comment explaining why status was deliberately not
  selected gets rewritten.
  - packing_items / prep_items selects gain sort_order, so "highest + 1" can be
  computed from state.
  - Event reshape becomes { id, sort_order, text, status, options }; nestItems
  items become { id, name, checked, sort_order }.

  New exported functions:

  function: setEventStatus(id, status)
  does: update({ status }) on itinerary_events .eq('id', id)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  function: deleteEvent(id)
  does: delete() on itinerary_events .eq('id', id)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  function: addEvent(date, text, sortOrder)
  does: inserts { date, sort_order, text, status: 'confirmed', options: null },
  returns the created row reshaped to { id, sort_order, text, status,  options 
  }
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  function: deletePackingItem(groupId, itemId) / deletePrepItem(…)
  does: wrappers over private deleteItem(table, …)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  function: addPackingItem(groupId, name, sortOrder) / addPrepItem(…)
  does: wrappers over private addItem(table, …); returns { id, name, checked, 
  sort_order }

  New private helpers: deleteItem(table, groupId, itemId), addItem(table, 
  groupId, name, sortOrder), newItemId(name) (slug + 4-char base36 suffix), and
  requireOneRow(table, data, label) factored out of the existing zero-row guard
  so all five write paths share it. Every write ends in .select() and goes
  through that guard. setItemChecked and the two set*Checked wrappers are
  untouched.

  Id generation lives here, not in the components — it's a uniqueness concern
  belonging to the data layer, and it keeps the slug logic from being written
  twice.

  src/App.jsx

  - Badge renders from event.status: 'open' → badge-candidate + OPEN,
  'confirmed' → badge-confirmed + CONFIRMED. The options list renders below
  whenever event.options exists, independent of status — so a confirmed event
  keeps showing its candidates.
  - New state: editError, addingDate, draft, saving.
  - One shared helper withEventsPatched(itinerary, date, updater) for immutable
  nested updates; toggle, delete, and add all go through it.
  - toggleStatus(date, event) — optimistic flip, setEventStatus, revert +
  editError on failure.
  - removeEvent(date, event) — window.confirm(\Delete '${event.text}'?`), then 
  await deleteEvent`, then drop from state.
  - addEventToDay(date, events) — sort_order = Math.max(0, ...events.map(e => 
  e.sort_order)) + 1, await addEvent, append the returned row (with its real
  database id) to state.
  - New markup inside each day: a × button per event after the badge, and an + 
  Add / inline input at the bottom of .day-body. Plus {editError && <p>Could not
  save: {editError}</p>} after the Itinerary <h2>.
  - Untouched: flights, hotels, header, tile strip, cityClass, formatStatus, day
  card structure and all existing class names.

  src/components/PackingList.jsx and TripPrep.jsx

  Identical treatment, each wired to its own three writers.

  - The separate checked map and itemKey go away; the component now owns const 
  [groupList, setGroupList] = useState(() => groups) with checked living on each
  item. Add and delete change the item list itself, so a single state tree is
  the only way to avoid two sources of truth drifting — a stale key in a
  parallel map after a delete is exactly the bug I'd rather not write.
  - Three local immutable helpers: withItemPatch, withItemRemoved,
  withItemAppended.
  - toggleItem keeps the optimistic flip/revert, now patching groupList instead
  of a map.
  - removeItem — confirm, await delete, drop from state.
  - addItem — sort_order = Math.max(0, ...items.map(i => i.sort_order)) + 1,
  await, append.
  - Markup: <li className="packing-row"> wrapping the existing .packing-item
  button plus a sibling × button (the delete control cannot go inside that
  button — nested interactive elements are invalid HTML), and an + Add / input
  at the group's bottom. The existing error line already sits after the <h2> and
  now covers all three failure kinds.

  Four decisions I want your call on

  The badge stays a <span> with role="button", tabIndex={0}, and Enter/Space 
  handling — not a real <button>. A <button> is UA-styled display: inline-block,
  which makes its vertical padding grow the line box, so every itinerary row
  would get taller. Keeping the span guarantees zero layout change while
  remaining keyboard-operable. If you'd rather have the correct element and
  accept a small row-height shift, say so and I'll switch it.

  I can't exercise the real confirm dialog. A native confirm() blocks the
  browser-automation channel entirely — it would hang the extension mid-test.
  I'll stub window.confirm = () => true in the page to drive the delete path,
  which means the dialog's own wiring gets one manual click from you rather than
  verification from me. The alternative is a custom in-page confirm, which
  isn't what you asked for.

  After a successful save the input closes. You specified Enter-saves and
  Escape/empty-blur-cancels but not what happens post-save. Closing is simpler
  to reason about; if you'd rather it stay open for rapid entry, that's a
  one-line change.

  Duplication between the two checklist components roughly doubles — three
  helpers plus three handlers each. A shared hook or one generic Checklist
  component is the right answer, but both need a fifth file and your fence names
  four. Flagging it as the follow-up rather than silently accruing it.

      191 +function newItemId(name) {                                    
      192 +  const slug = name                                           
      193 +    .normalize('NFD')                                         
      194 +    .replace(/[̀-ͯ]/g, '')

      200 +  return `${slug || 'item'}-${suffix}`

> Role: You are conducting a usability audit as a senior product designer       
  would. This is a read-only engagement: change no files, write no code. Your   
  deliverable is a report.                                                      
                                                                                
  Context — the product and its users:                                          
                                                                                
  Personal trip dashboard for a family trip to Portugal, Aug 28–Sep 6. Live on  
  Vercel, data in Supabase.                                                     
  Two users. Me: built the app, knows every affordance, uses it for planning    
  now and reference during the trip. My wife: will start using it on her phone  
  soon — glance-oriented, mid-trip usage ("what's today, what's left to pack"), 
  no involvement in building the app, zero training. Judge all affordance and   
  discoverability questions against her, not me. Both of us may check, toggle,  
  add, and delete — sometimes concurrently from two phones. A native iOS        
  version for both of us is planned for the fall.                               
  Primary context of use during the trip: iPhone Safari, often one-handed,      
  quick glances — "what's the plan today / what's next / what's still           
  undecided." Hotel and cellular wifi, sometimes poor.                          
  Secondary context, pre-trip: desktop and phone, planning mode — loading       
  packing/prep lists, confirming open decisions, cleaning up data using the     
  editing controls.                                                             
  Features, complete as of slice 4: itinerary by day (status badge tap-toggles  
  open/confirmed; options lists under events; × delete; + Add per day), packing 
  and prep checklists (check/uncheck, × delete, + Add per group),               
  flights/hotels display-only.                                                  
  Known constraints: hand-written CSS, no component library, no routing (one    
  page). The trip departs Aug 28 — that date is immovable, but it caps          
  sequencing, not ambition: recommend what's right, and sort it by when it      
  should land.                                                                  
                                                                                
  Task — audit the app at two viewports: desktop, and mobile at ~390px width    
  (use the browser tools; actually render and measure, don't reason from the    
  code alone). Evaluate against:                                                
                                                                                
  The during-trip job: how many scrolls/taps from open to "today"? Is today     
  distinguishable at a glance? How does the app serve a 10-second glance versus 
  a 5-minute planning session?                                                  
  Mobile ergonomics: touch-target sizes vs. the 44px guideline (measure the     
  badge, ×, + Add, checkboxes), thumb-reach for common actions, accidental-tap  
  risk (× adjacency to other controls), the known iOS sticky tap-highlight      
  issue.                                                                        
  Information density and scrolling on xs — where does vertical space go, and   
  what would recover it without hiding information?                             
  Editing affordance discoverability: would the second user know badges are     
  tappable and rows deletable? Is destructive action appropriately guarded?     
  Status communication: loading, save-in-flight, save-failed, offline. What     
  does the user see when a write is slow or the connection drops — and what     
  happens when two phones edit concurrently?                                    
  Standard heuristics: visibility of system status, error recovery,             
  consistency, and accessibility basics (contrast, focus visibility, roles) —   
  briefly, not as the centerpiece.                                              
                                                                                
  Output contract — a report and nothing else:                                  
                                                                                
  Each finding: severity (blocker / major / minor / polish), evidence (measured 
  numbers or observed behavior — screenshots/DOM measurements where possible),  
  smallest smart fix (the minimal change that resolves it — name the pattern,   
  e.g. "collapsible sections," not a redesign), effort (S/M/L), and type        
  (CSS-only / layout / behavior).                                               
  State-model recommendation (its own section): For each category of state —    
  itinerary event status, packing checkmarks, prep checkmarks, adds/deletes —   
  recommend whether one shared household state or per-user state better serves  
  these two users, with rationale grounded in their actual jobs (does her       
  checkmark answer his question, or pollute it?). If any surface warrants       
  per-user state, spell out what it costs: user identity (the app currently has 
  none), schema changes, and sync/merge complexity — and say when it should be  
  built: before the trip, or in the fall native scope. A recommendation of      
  "shared everywhere" is acceptable if the reasoning supports it; don't invent  
  per-user complexity to seem thorough.                                         
  Then a triage sorted by when it should land: (A) before the trip — worth      
  building and testing in the remaining runway, (B) before the trip only if     
  cheap, (C) after the trip / belongs in the native iOS version — anything      
  better solved by native patterns goes to C, not into web CSS. For anything    
  recommended to land in the final days before Aug 28, say so explicitly and    
  note that it compresses real-use testing time.                                
  Close with a proposed scope for one implementation prompt covering A plus the 
  cheapest of B — and explicitly list what you are recommending against doing   
  now.                                                                          
  No visual rebranding — the Portugal palette and typography stay. Solutions    
  should be smart, and may be ambitious where the finding justifies it.

● Usability Audit — Portugal Trip Dashboard

  Method. Rendered and measured the live app at two viewports: desktop (1427×698
  CSS px, 880px content column) and mobile at exactly 390×844 via a same-origin
  iframe — Chrome clamps window width above 390, so the iframe was the only way
  to get the real mobile media-query branch. All numbers below are measured
  from the rendered DOM, not read off the CSS. No files were changed.

  Two measurement caveats, stated up front: dark mode contrast is computed from
  the design tokens rather than rendered, because prefers-color-scheme can't be
  emulated through these tools; and iOS sticky-hover is inferred from the CSS
  rule set plus the measured default tap-highlight, since this is desktop
  Chrome, not Safari on a phone.

  One non-finding, so it isn't mistaken for one: the load fires 16 API requests,
  each table twice. That's StrictMode in src/main.jsx:7 double-invoking the
  effect in dev. Production fires 8.

  ---
  1. The during-trip job

  F1 — There is no concept of "today." — blocker
  Measured: [class*=today], [data-today] → 0 elements. At 390px the first screen
  contains the header and Bookings only; no itinerary content is above the 
  fold. The first day card starts at y=889 (1.05 screens down), and mid-trip
  days sit far below: Sep 2 at 4.1 screens, Sep 4 at 5.5, Sep 5 at 6.0, Sep 6 at
  6.6. To answer "what's today" she scrolls ~4 screens and reads dates to find
  her place.

  This is the primary job and the app does not serve it. Everything else in this
  report is secondary.

  Smallest smart fix: today anchor — compute today's date, mark that card
  (aria-current="date" plus a TODAY label and accent), and scrollIntoView it
  once data lands. Before Aug 28 anchor to day 1; after Sep 6, to the last day.
  Effort S. Behavior + minor CSS.

  F2 — Bookings occupy the entire first screen. — major
  Measured: 661px tall (8.4% of the document), sitting above the itinerary.
  Flights and hotels are display-only reference needed twice in ten days, yet
  they are the first thing both users see on every open.

  Fix: move Bookings below the Itinerary, or collapse to a one-line summary.
  Effort S. Layout.

  F3 — Scroll position is lost on every reload. — major
  Measured: scrolled to y=3489 (Sep 2), reloaded, scrollY came back 0 with no
  day card visible at all. Native scroll restoration can't work because the
  Loading trip… state collapses the document to a few hundred pixels before the
  real content arrives. During the trip she will reload constantly — flaky hotel
  wifi, returning to Safari from other apps.

  Fix: subsumed by F1. Restoring today is better than restoring an arbitrary
  offset. Effort S (same change). Behavior.

  F4 — Loading is all-or-nothing. — major
  Measured: 8 GETs issued in parallel (max start spread <50ms), slowest 244ms,
  wall time 249ms on good wifi, all gated behind a single Promise.all. One slow
  table blanks the entire app, including the itinerary. No service worker
  (serviceWorker.controller → false), no cache — offline shows nothing.

  Fix: stage the load so the itinerary paints as soon as its three queries
  resolve. Separately, "stale-while-revalidate from localStorage" — persist the
  last good fetch, render it instantly, revalidate behind it — is the genuinely
  valuable version for a trip. Effort M. Behavior.

  F5 — A failed load replaces the whole page with one bare sentence and no way 
  to retry. — major
  On poor wifi this is the state she will actually meet. There is no retry, and
  no fallback to last-known data.

  Fix: keep last-good data when available; otherwise show the message plus a Try
  again button. Effort S. Behavior.

  Glance vs. planning. The 10-second glance is currently unserved: no today, no
  "what's next," no "what's undecided" summary, 9.4 screens of document. The
  5-minute planning session is well served — every affordance he needs is
  present and direct. The asymmetry is the core problem, and it maps exactly
  onto the two users.

  ---
  2. Mobile ergonomics at 390px

  F6 — Every interactive control fails the 44px guideline. — major

  ┌──────────────────┬──────────────────────┬───────────────────┬──────────┐
  │     Control      │       Measured       │     vs. 44×44     │ Failing  │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────┼───────────────────┼──────────┤
  │ Status badge     │ 76.5 × 18.4          │ 42% of min height │ 63 of 63 │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────┼───────────────────┼──────────┤
  │ × delete         │ 16.3 × 15.0 = 245px² │ 13% of 1936px²    │ 83 of 83 │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────┼───────────────────┼──────────┤
  │ + Add            │ 35 × 18.4 = 644px²   │ 33%               │ 16 of 16 │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────┼───────────────────┼──────────┤
  │ Packing/prep row │ 325 × 40.4           │ just under        │ 10 of 20 │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────┼───────────────────┼──────────┤
  │ Add input        │ — × 34.4             │ under             │ 1 of 1   │
  └──────────────────┴──────────────────────┴───────────────────┴──────────┘

  Fix: invisible hit-area expansion — padding on badge/×/+ Add with compensating
  negative margins, min-height: 44px on rows. Visual size unchanged. Effort 
  S–M. Mostly CSS-only.

  F7 — Destructive and non-destructive controls are adjacent or overlapping. — 
  major
  Measured: in itinerary rows the gap from badge right edge to × left edge is
  4.2px. In packing rows the gap is −6.0px — the × literally overlaps the item
  button's box (a consequence of .packing-item { margin: 0 -6px }), making the
  button's last 6px unreachable.

  The asymmetry is what makes this serious: the unguarded control is the one
  that's easy to hit. Mis-tapping × opens a named confirm she can decline.
  Mis-tapping the badge silently rewrites trip data with no confirm, no undo,
  and no feedback beyond an 11px label changing.

  This interacts with F6 and they must ship together. Expanding both hit areas
  while they sit 4.2px apart makes mis-taps worse, not better. The minimal
  correct version: push × to the row's right edge with ≥12px separation, then
  expand.

  Fix (minimal): right-align × with real separation + expand. Fix (better, 
  later): remove × from the default view behind a per-section Edit toggle.
  Effort M. Layout + behavior.

  F8 — iOS Safari will zoom the page every time she taps the add input. — major
  Measured: .add-input computed font-size 14px; anything under 16px triggers
  auto-zoom on focus in iOS Safari, and it does not zoom back out. She'll be
  left at a zoomed viewport mid-list. Viewport meta is correct (device-width,
  zoom not blocked) — do not "fix" this by disabling user zoom.

  Fix: font-size: 16px. Effort S. CSS-only. Highest ratio of annoyance-removed
  to characters-typed in this report.

  F9 — Sticky hover / tap highlight on iOS. — minor
  Measured: 4 :hover rules (.packing-item:hover changes background; badge/×/+ 
  Add change color), and -webkit-tap-highlight-color is the browser default
  rgba(0,0,0,0.18) with no override anywhere. On iOS, tap applies :hover and it
  persists until the next tap elsewhere — a row she just tapped stays shaded,
  reading as "selected."

  Fix: wrap hover rules in @media (hover: hover); set an explicit tap-highlight.
  Effort S. CSS-only.

  Thumb reach. Fine. × sits 37.6px from the right edge, comfortably in the thumb
  arc; rows span the full width. No action.

  ---
  3. Density and scrolling on xs

  Document is 7903px = 9.4 screens at 390×844. Where it goes, and what's
  recoverable without hiding information:

  ┌────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────┬──────────────────────┐
  │        Consumer        │        Measured         │         Note         │
  ├────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │ Option lists           │ 942px (13 lists, 36     │ Always expanded      │
  │                        │ rows)                   │                      │
  ├────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │ "Sunset ritual" rows   │ 614px (8 identical      │ Same sentence, 8×    │
  │                        │ rows)                   │                      │
  ├────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │ Bookings section       │ 661px                   │ F2                   │
  ├────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │ + Add rows in          │ 248px                   │ 10 × ~25px           │
  │ itinerary              │                         │                      │
  ├────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │ "Free day in…" rows    │ 163px                   │ 6 rows saying        │
  │                        │                         │ nothing              │
  ├────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │ Header                 │ 143px                   │ Fine                 │
  └────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────┴──────────────────────┘

  F10 — ~20% of the document is recoverable. — major
  Options (942px) + sunset ritual (614px) = 1556px, about a fifth of total
  scroll.

  Fix (a): collapse option lists to a tappable count — Dinner · 6 options ▸. The
  information stays one tap away, and this is also the right glance semantics:
  the glance wants "Dinner — undecided," not six restaurant names. Effort M. 
  Layout + behavior.
  Fix (b): the sunset ritual is a trip-wide ritual stored as 8 duplicate rows.
  Cheapest honest fix is to leave the data alone and collapse the presentation —
  render it once at the top of the itinerary, or as a small per-day glyph.
  Effort M.

  ---
  4. Editing affordance discoverability

  F12 — Nothing signals that badges are tappable, and the visual language 
  actively misleads. — major
  The status badge is styled by the same .badge rule as the BOOKED badges on
  flights and hotels, which are not interactive. Same shape, same size, same
  font — different behavior. The only interactivity cues are cursor: pointer
  (invisible on touch) and a hover border-color shift (unavailable on touch).
  Judged against her, with zero training: she has no way to learn that OPEN can
  be tapped, and no way to guess that BOOKED can't.

  Fix: give interactive badges a persistent glyph the static ones lack — OPEN ▸,
  or a small chevron/dot. Effort S. CSS-only plus a character of markup.

  F13 — The × is effectively invisible. — minor (discoverability) / fails a11y
  Measured contrast 1.51:1 (--line #ddd0ac on #fffdf7), below the 3:1 non-text
  minimum. Quiet was the intent, and quiet is defensible for a destructive
  control — but 1.51:1 is past quiet into undiscoverable, while still being
  large enough to hit by accident. That combination is the worst of both.
  Folding into F7's edit-mode answer resolves it properly: absent by default,
  clearly visible at proper contrast when editing.

  F14 — Delete guarding is right; toggle guarding is the gap. — minor
  window.confirm("Delete 'Ramiro'?") names the item and renders as a real system
  sheet on iOS. Verified on all three lists: declining leaves every row intact.
  Keep it.

  The toggle, by contrast, is unguarded, silent, has no undo, and is the largest
  and most mis-tappable target on the row. A confirm dialog on a two-state
  toggle would be worse than the problem — the fix is F6/F7 geometry, not a
  prompt.

  F23 — The add input has an ambiguous unsaved state. — minor
  Enter saves; Escape cancels; blur with empty text cancels; blur with text does
  nothing — the input stays open holding unsaved text with no indication and no
  Save affordance. On iOS she may well tap away expecting it to commit. This is
  current spec'd behavior, so changing it is a deliberate recommendation.

  Fix: save on blur when non-empty, or add a small ✓. Effort S. Behavior.

  ---
  5. Status communication, failure, and concurrency

  F16 — A hanging write is silently lost, and the UI claims success. — blocker
  Observed directly during slice-4 verification: PATCHes sat pending for
  minutes; the optimistic flip stayed on screen; no error appeared; the change
  never reached the database; a reload silently reverted it. Root cause: the
  revert-and-report path only fires on rejection, and fetch has no default
  timeout, so a stall is indistinguishable from success.

  An app that says "packed" when nothing was saved is worse than one that says
  "failed," and she has no reason to distrust it. This is the most important
  finding in the report.

  Fix: AbortController with an ~8s timeout on all five write functions. A stall
  becomes a rejection, which the existing revert + error path already handles
  correctly. Effort S. Behavior. Best value-per-effort here by a wide margin.

  F15 — No save-in-flight indication. — major
  Optimistic flip is instant and identical whether the write took 60ms or is
  still outstanding. On poor wifi there is no way to tell.

  Fix: per-row pending state — subtle opacity or a small spinner until
  resolution. Effort M. Behavior + CSS.

  F17 — Error messages appear up to 5.6 screens from the action. — major
  Measured: the itinerary error line renders at y=847; the last day card is at
  y=5611 — 4764px apart. Fail a save while editing Sep 5 and the explanation is
  six screens above; all she sees is a row quietly changing back. Also 0 
  aria-live regions, so nothing is announced.

  Fix: one fixed toast for save failures, with aria-live="polite". Effort M. 
  Layout + behavior.

  F18 — Two phones diverge silently and indefinitely. — major
  Measured: zero network requests across 18 seconds of idle. Data is fetched
  once at mount; no polling, no revalidation, no realtime channel. Concrete
  consequences for two concurrent editors:

  - Last-write-wins per column — his toggle can be overwritten by her tap
  carrying stale state, invisibly to both.
  - If he deletes a row still on her screen, her toggle hits the zero-row guard
  and she sees raw developer text: packing_items — no row matched ilya/jeans.
  - She can add an item he just added; nothing dedupes.

  Severity stops at major rather than blocker: two users, low-stakes data, and
  mostly non-overlapping editing in time.

  Fix (cheap, pre-trip): refetch on visibilitychange/focus. This matches the
  real pattern — phone goes in pocket, comes back out. Plus humanize the
  zero-row message to "This item was removed on another device." Effort S. 
  Behavior.
  Fix (right, later): Supabase Realtime subscriptions — native scope or
  post-trip.

  ---
  6. Heuristics and accessibility, briefly

  F19 — Contrast is marginal across the board, and fails in three places. — 
  minor

  ┌────────────────────────────────────────┬──────────┬─────────────────────┐
  │                Element                 │ Measured │         AA          │
  ├────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ Event text, badge-OPEN, + Add, day     │ 4.45:1   │ needs 4.5 —         │
  │ date, option text                      │          │ marginal fail       │
  ├────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ badge-confirmed (11px)                 │ 3.21:1   │ fail                │
  ├────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ ×, .day-index                          │ 1.51:1   │ fail (3:1 non-text) │
  └────────────────────────────────────────┴──────────┴─────────────────────┘

  A token tweak — darken --ink-soft slightly, --gold for the confirmed badge —
  clears all the text cases. Not rebranding; the palette identity is unaffected.
  Dark mode deserves the same pass. Effort S. CSS-only.

  F20 — Focus visibility is weak. — polish. Only three selectors have
  :focus-visible, each shifting a color or border rather than showing a ring.
  .packing-item has none and relies on the UA default. Fix: one shared
  :focus-visible { outline: 2px solid var(--azulejo); outline-offset: 2px }. S. 
  CSS-only.

  F21 — The add input has no accessible name. — polish. Placeholder only. Fix:
  aria-label. S.

  F22 — One section is inconsistent. — polish. #trip-prep is styled with inline
  styles duplicating what #bookings, #itinerary-section, and #packing-list get
  from a shared rule — the only section outside it, a leftover from the
  append-only CSS constraint in an earlier slice. Fix: add it to the shared
  selector, drop the inline style. S. CSS-only.

  Roles are otherwise sound: role="button" + tabIndex + Enter/Space on badges,
  aria-pressed on checkboxes, aria-label on every ×, sensible h1/h2 structure,
  zoom not blocked.

  ---
  7. State-model recommendation

  Recommendation: shared state everywhere. Build no per-user state — not before 
  the trip, and not in the fall unless real use produces a need.

  The reasoning is one distinction: every checkbox and badge in this app records
  a fact about the physical world, not an opinion. Per-user state earns its
  complexity when the state is a personal view. Here it never is.

  Category: Itinerary event status
  Recommendation: Shared
  Why: "Is dinner decided?" is a household fact about a reservation. A table is
  booked or it isn't; two independent answers would be incoherent. Her
  confirming answers his question.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Category: Prep checkmarks
  Recommendation: Shared
  Why: Single-owner logistics tasks — "call JNcQUOI," "seat assignments." The
  question is "has anyone done this." Per-user would make doneness ambiguous.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Category: Adds / deletes
  Recommendation: Shared
  Why: These lists are shared documents. A private itinerary event is a
  different
  product.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Category: Packing checkmarks
  Recommendation: Shared
  Why: The interesting case — and it resolves the same way.

  On packing specifically, since that's where per-user intuition pulls hardest.
  The groups are already per-person — Ilya / Elina / Kids — so ownership is
  encoded in the data's shape, not needed in the checkbox. Realistically he
  packs his group, she packs hers and the kids'. Her ticking "Kids / Passport"
  answers a shared question; his ticking "Ilya / Jeans" is nobody else's
  business but also nobody else's contradiction. Two people ticking the same
  item independently would be meaningless: the jeans are in the bag or they
  aren't. Her checkmark answers his question; it does not pollute it.

  The need that looks like per-user state is actually filtering. She wants "my
  stuff and the kids' stuff" without scrolling past his. That is a per-device UI
  preference — which groups are collapsed — belonging in localStorage, not a
  per-user data axis. Cheap, and it should be built with the
  collapsible-sections work in F10.

  What per-user would cost, spelled out, so the decision is informed rather than
  assumed:

  - Identity. The app has none — no auth, no user concept, no session. Real
  per-user state requires Supabase Auth. The shortcut (a locally chosen "who am
  I") is fake identity: it breaks the moment she opens the app on a second
  device, and it can't be trusted for merges.
  - Schema. checked stops being a column and becomes item_checks(group_id, 
  item_id, user_id, checked). Every read becomes a join or a second query plus a
  client-side reduction; every write carries a user id; the composite key grows
  a third member.
  - Sync and merge. Two axes of divergence instead of one. "Is it packed"
  becomes a reduction over users and you must choose the rule — any, or all —
  and then explain that choice in the UI.
  - Security theatre. RLS is off today. Per-user rows in a two-person household
  immediately raise "can she see his checks?" — and the answer is yes, she
  should. So RLS would protect nothing and cost ceremony.

  Timing. None of it. The state work worth doing before Aug 28 is making the
  shared model trustworthy: F16 (timeout, so "saved" means saved) and F18
  (revalidate on focus, so two phones converge). Those two are what actually
  make shared state work for two concurrent users. Realtime sync is the natural
  fall/native item; per-user state is not on the roadmap at all until use
  demands it.

  ---
  8. Triage by when it should land

  (A) Before the trip — worth building and testing in the 19-day runway

  (C) After the trip / native iOS scope

  - F4 progressive loading and offline cache — a native app gets a local store
  and real sync; a service worker is a poor investment for a ten-day trip
  - F18b Supabase Realtime live sync
  - Undo for deletes — a native toast-with-undo, not a web undo stack
  - Swipe-to-delete — a native gesture; do not emulate it in CSS
  - Pull-to-refresh — native
  - Per-user state — recommended against entirely

  Explicit sequencing warning. Anything landing after roughly Aug 24 leaves
  under four days of real use before departure, and the last of those days are
  travel days. The A list is deliberately sized to fit well inside that. F7-full
  (edit mode) is the one item I would not start after Aug 22: it changes how
  both users interact with every row in the app, and it is precisely the kind of
  change that needs a week of ordinary use to shake out. If it slips, ship the
  minimal F7 (separate and right-align ×) and leave edit mode to the native
  version.

  ---
  9. Proposed scope for one implementation prompt

  "Trust and glance" — all of A plus the cheapest of B.

  1. Write timeout. AbortController, ~8s, on all five write functions in
  tripData.js. Stalls become rejections the existing revert-and-report path
  already handles.
  2. Today anchor. Mark today's day card (aria-current="date", TODAY label,
  accent that reuses the existing city-accent pattern); scroll it into view once
  data lands. Before Aug 28 → day 1; after Sep 6 → last day.
  3. Revalidate on focus. Refetch on visibilitychange/focus and replace state.
  Rewrite no row matched as "This item was removed on another device."
  4. Retry on load failure. Keep the error line, add a Try again button.
  5. One save-failure toast, fixed position, aria-live="polite", replacing the
  three section-top error lines.
  6. Touch targets, done as one change: right-align × at the row edge with ≥12px
  separation (fixing the −6px packing overlap), then expand badge, ×, and + Add
  hit areas to ≥44px via padding and compensating margins; min-height: 44px on
  rows. Visual sizes unchanged.
  7. iOS input hygiene. .add-input to 16px; aria-label; save on blur when
  non-empty.
  8. CSS hygiene. Shared :focus-visible ring; hover rules behind @media (hover: 
  hover); explicit -webkit-tap-highlight-color; darken --ink-soft and --gold to
  clear 4.5:1 in both light and dark; × to ≥3:1.
  9. Move Bookings below the Itinerary.
  10. #trip-prep joins the shared section rule, inline styles removed.

  That is one coherent slice: it makes the app trustworthy (1, 3, 4, 5), answers
  "what's today" (2, 9), and makes it usable with a thumb (6, 7, 8).

  Explicitly recommending against, now:

  - Per-user state, user identity, or auth in any form
  - Realtime subscriptions
  - Service worker or offline caching
  - The full edit-mode row redesign (F7-full) — unless it can land by ~Aug 22
  - Swipe gestures, undo stacks, routing or multi-page, any component library
  - Collapsible option lists and the sunset-ritual de-duplication — real wins,
  but M-effort each and they touch itinerary rendering that item 6 is already
  changing; sequence them after A is in real use
  - Any visual rebranding — out of scope by instruction, and the palette is not
  the problem

  One process note. Two of the A items — the write timeout and the concurrency
  behavior — are the hardest things to verify through browser automation in this
  environment, as slice 4 demonstrated. Both deserve a deliberate manual test
  on an actual iPhone with wifi toggled off mid-tap, and a two-phone test where
  you both edit the same row. Budget an evening for that rather than trusting an
  automated pass.

● Plan below. Three verified facts shaped it: .abortSignal() exists in
  postgrest-js 2.112.2; dark mode already clears 4.5:1 (ink-soft 5.17, gold
  5.08), so only the light palette needs tokens touched; and one correction to
  my own audit — the confirmed badge measures 2.78:1, not the 3.21 I reported,
  because I measured it against the card background instead of its own
  translucent --gold-bg fill. It needs more darkening than the audit implied.

  src/lib/tripData.js

  Item 1 — timeout. New WRITE_TIMEOUT_MS = 8000 and one shared wrapper all seven
  write paths route through:

  async function runWrite(table, label, buildQuery) {
    if (!supabase) throw new Error(missingEnvMessage)
    const controller = new AbortController()
    const timer = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), WRITE_TIMEOUT_MS)
    let result
    try {
      result = await buildQuery().abortSignal(controller.signal)
    } finally {
      clearTimeout(timer)
    }
    if (controller.signal.aborted) throw new Error('Timed out after 8s — check 
  your connection.')
    if (result.error) throw new Error(`${table} — ${result.error.message}`)
    requireOneRow(table, result.data, label)
    return result.data
  }

  Covers setEventStatus, deleteEvent, addEvent, removeEventOption,
  setItemChecked, deleteItem, addItem. Reads stay untimed — see the flag at the
  end.

  Item 3 — message. requireOneRow throws 'This item was removed on another 
  device.' (no table prefix; it's user-facing).

  Item 12 — the new write. Exact signature:

  /**
   * Removes one option from an event's options array. Status is never touched.
   * @param {number}   id       itinerary_events.id
   * @param {string[]} options  the event's current options, from UI state
   * @param {string}   option   the option to remove
   * @returns {Promise<string[]|null>} the value actually persisted
   */
  export async function removeEventOption(id, options, option)

  Filters option out, writes next.length ? next : null, returns that value so
  the caller stores exactly what the database holds. Read-modify-write on a
  jsonb array, so it inherits the app's existing last-write-wins behavior; two
  phones removing different options in the same second will lose one. Consistent
  with every other write here, and item 3's revalidation narrows the window.
